in writing. if a country in europe's neighbourhood meets the eu's political and economic requirements, there should be no obstacles to membership. the eu is not a cultural or religious bloc, but a cooperation in which the member states guarantee democracy, human rights and a functioning market economy. therefore, turkish membership cannot be rejected on religious or geographical grounds. the junilist therefore believes that turkey should be able to become a member of the eu in the long term. but the eu has not been reformed enough to accept turkey as a member. this is particularly true of agricultural policy and the structural funds. turkey has also failed to meet eu policy requirements to a sufficient extent. several human rights requirements are not implemented. the armenian genocide of 1915 has not been recognized, nor has the sovereignty of cyprus. the financial implications of turkey's accession to the eu are still uncertain. finally, the draft constitutional treaty has now fallen and it is therefore completely unknown how the eu will be governed in the future. this is another important reason for postponing the accession negotiations. in view of this, we do not think that accession negotiations should be opened at this stage. that is why we are voting against the resolution as a whole.
in writing. i voted against or abstained on several parts of this report which were related to parliament's competence. there were unnecessary references to the constitution, which is now dead. i believe that a certain degree of freedom to have one's own fiscal policy is important, not least in order to be able to contribute to credit guarantee societies in rural areas which can create cooperation at local level. i abstained on those points where the demands on the working conditions of parliament were perfectly reasonable but an unnecessary reference to the constitution was smuggled in.
in writing. europe needs railways, mainly for environmental reasons but also for freight and passenger transport. this requires the opening up of railway markets between countries, so that trains can cross borders quickly and smoothly. the markets for rail freight are now being opened up under the second railway package, which we think is good. the jarzembowski report now proposes that passenger services, both international and domestic, should also be opened according to a certain timetable. we believe that international passenger traffic can be restored within a few years. but as far as national passenger traffic is concerned, we believe that this must be done over a longer period of time than is proposed. that is why we are not prepared today to vote in favour of mr jarzembowski's proposal for the liberalization of national passenger transport, since we cannot see the consequences of his proposal, for example, for local and regional public transport.
in writing. the junilista group opposes the commission's proposal to create common rules for train drivers and staff. cooperation must be based on trust and intergovernmental agreements. we therefore abstain on all amendments and would have liked to have had the opportunity to vote against the commission's original text.
in writing. there is an intergovernmental organisation for international rail transport (otif) which has already agreed on passenger rights. there is a charter of quality standards for rail passenger transport signed by the european railways. we do not believe that the eu should intervene and regulate this area. if regulation is needed in this area, it will be done through the national authorities of the respective countries. in this context, voting for a regulation on minimum compensation for delayed travellers is a major step forward. this may lead to increased costs for service providers to their passengers, which may further hit the industry hard in a situation where it is pressured by low-cost flights and buses. we therefore believe that the european parliament's report does not propose anything that will benefit travellers in the long term. we therefore vote against parliament's amendments to the commission's proposal. we also want to reject the commission's proposal.
in writing. we do not think that there is a need for an eu framework for compensation in the event of delay in the transport of goods by rail. we therefore support the position of the committee on transport and reject the commission's proposal. the commission's proposal is intended to stimulate railway undertakings to improve the quality of freight transport and thus to increase customer demand. we question whether the proposed regulations are desirable and whether they will have the intended effect.
in writing. we swedish social democrats have decided to abstain on the question of a stronger partnership for the outermost regions. many regions in europe have special needs and difficulties due to long distances from commercial centres, glaciation or difficult terrain and climatic conditions. the islands and island groups in the outermost regions have recognised disadvantages and we support the eu's policy in this area. the partnership with the eu also entails obligations to fish carefully, protect our common environment and use eu support money responsibly. we think that the marques report goes too far in calling for more aid money for agriculture and fisheries. that is why we have decided to abstain.
in writing. the junilist group votes against these reports because they are two new examples of how the european parliament wants to give the eu new areas of competence through own-initiative reports. the commission is also proposing to introduce a territorial dimension, with the aim of clarifying how the member states should manage the relations between the centre and the periphery. marques calls for more support for fisheries, agriculture, banana production and so on to further promote peripheral areas within the union. the report highlights the importance of additional support for the fishing industry, including through efforts to modernise the fishing fleet to increase its profitability. these two reports also lead to new expenditure and sanction areas of aid which should be reduced rather than extended.
mr president, mr president of the council and mr commissioner! as has been said here, the transport sector accounts for the absolute majority of total oil consumption in the eu. we are all extremely dependent on oil to transport goods and to move us, because road transport is so dominant. this is something that we and the industry have known for a long time, but the consciousness has not been acted upon. we must now shift our focus to renewable energy sources and fuels. but there are also positive trends, awareness is growing and the automotive industry in the united states has asked the president to convene a summit after katrina to discuss what can be done to develop alternative fuels. the major automakers also say that the united states must reduce its dependence on gasoline. we must use this awareness to create the conditions in our societies and in our union, and that is what we as politicians can do. in the field of transport, we must research new renewable fuels, such as synthetic diesel. we need to promote alternative fuels, like ethanol. we need to renew the fleet, and here we can do quite a lot, quite quickly. we also need to look at alternative transportation. we have talked about railways today and decided to develop rail transport. this is important, but we must also write more in the white paper review and take more decisions on alternative fuels and the renewal of the car fleet.
for the ind/dem group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. the junilista group is a friend of the un and believes that this organisation has a good capacity to contribute to constructive solutions to international conflicts. we do not, however, believe that the eu and its parliament should dictate how the un should work and what the organisation's objectives should be. the debate on the future of the un must be conducted between the un member states and in a wider international context than the european one. we are critical of the proposal that the eu should be represented by a common seat on the un security council. we also do not support parliament's wish to set up joint eu delegations at the various un headquarters. eu member states have different views on the issues dealt with by the un and its security council. sweden, for example, has played an important role in the united nations as a bridge builder between poor and rich countries, as a mediator and as a promoter of disarmament. it shows that even small countries have an important role to play in the un and in international politics. we fear that the voices of the small countries will not be heard if the eu is to speak with one voice in the context of the un. which of the eu's 25 voices would then speak through this common space? if the eu could speak with one voice in the un, why are britain and france defending their current permanent seats on the security council? why is germany seeking a seat on the security council? the truth is that eu countries do not share a common view on international political issues. this has been demonstrated on several occasions, not least in connection with the us intervention in iraq. let us embrace the multifaceted europe and let us work to ensure that all voices are heard in the debate. give it up.
mr president, council, commission, members and audience. the un summit made clear the need for cooperation against terrorism, climate change, international crime, migration and weapons of mass destruction and that more cooperation is needed, not less. the peacebuilding commission was given a lot of room. the european parliament is now able to follow up. following the initiative of the swedish foreign minister, with 13 female foreign ministers and commissioner ferrero-waldner, we now have the peace-prevention work to look at. we need to include more women and, above all, every other woman at all levels, so that gender representation enables women and men to do a better job. and why is that so important? yes, because in modern wars and conflicts, the vast majority of the victims are innocent women and children. the transition from conflict to sustainable peace requires all the resources and civil solutions. how do we follow up on this in the eu? when the mdgs were discussed, it became clear that donor countries had to be more generous. that is why we are very pleased to have heard this weekend that 18 countries have been granted debt relief. i regret that only sweden and four other countries are achieving 0.7% aid, we must do better. and by the year 2000, sweden will reach the level of 1 percent. with all the food we serve here in the eu, we should be able to fill the glass easily.
for the gue/ngl group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. i want to join the harsh criticism of the lukashenko regime here tonight. we have seen a serious deterioration of an already bad situation in belarus, and the regime is now increasingly showing the classic features of a political dictatorship. i can agree with almost all the points made in the resolution, but i think there is one area which could be developed further. this concerns the importance of the independent trade union movement in belarus. independent trade unions have been one of the key organisers of opposition to the lukashenko regime, but they are today fighting for their survival and existence under the oppression. there is a great deal of cooperation between trade unions in other eu countries, e.g. in sweden, and independent trade unions in belarus. i hope they will have the support of the european union for the democratic opposition. we have been reminded this week of the importance of solidarity in poland and it is not without significance that we have a strong and independent trade union movement to achieve the changes needed in belarus.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i would also like to thank the vatanen for this report and for the great work it has done. we are facing a serious problem, but i would like to point out that we are on the right track in europe. we have a lot of dead, way too many - we wouldn't accept any - but the death rate is going down. it is important that europe is a model in the global road safety effort, and we can be. in terms of technological progress, we are an example. if we get better cars in europe and in the world, we'll get our death rate down. we must use good examples, we have a proposal in amendment no 5 to encourage buyers of transport to demand road safety from those who sell transport. we can use legislation, for example on alcohol in traffic, because we know that you should never drive drunk in traffic. but i do not think that the legislative instrument should be used in the case of speeds, because the road surface can be very different and the condition of the roads different, so we must have a varied view of road safety. then we can set an example for the rest of the world.
in writing. the proposal for a developed partnership between the eu and india contains several good proposals, including the development of economic links between the continents and the strengthening of human rights. we are, however, opposed to the eu's threat to impose protectionist measures on india. this is done while the eu subsidises its own agricultural products dumped on the indian market. this leads to the elimination of local production, including milk. we cannot support the overall aim of the proposal to strengthen the eu as an international actor. in this context, the partnership is another way of strengthening the eu's global role, which undermines the foreign policy of the member states. that is why we are voting against the report as a whole.
in writing. the report contains many good proposals for improving road safety in europe. but we think that the eu's role in road safety is being overstated. the report points out that if all member states were to achieve the same results as the uk and sweden, the number of fatalities would fall by 17 000 a year in the eu-25. that would be a 39 percent reduction. it also notes that a campaign launched in france in 2002 has reduced the number of fatal accidents by 30 percent in two years. the individual member states can therefore improve their own road safety and are ultimately responsible for these matters. political responsibility lies with the national parliaments. this report goes too far when it even calls on the commission to promote education at primary school level in order to reduce the mortality rate among young people. it is the responsibility of the member states to integrate traffic education into the school curriculum. we are voting against this report because we believe that it is based on a faulty reasoning about which political level is responsible for road safety issues.
in writing. the moderates and the christian democrats have today voted against the own-initiative report on road safety. we believe that road safety in all its forms is of the utmost importance and that the report raises a number of urgent proposals. however, the reason for our position is that the report is largely dominated by issues which should be dealt with at national rather than eu level. an expansion of the eu's influence in the field of road safety would lead to unnecessary detail governments and more bureaucracy.
mr president, i would like to thank you. kinnock is right. africa doesn't need more guns. the strategy shows that more needs to be done. development policy will never succeed if trade policy sabotages it. i would also like to warn against an exaggerated belief that foreign capital and companies will solve africa's problems. we must support the development of locally owned businesses where the profits stay in africa. it is important that they have full freedom to choose their own path of development, free from pressure and forced market opening. they must decide for themselves what is in their best interests. a coherent approach between trade, development cooperation and democracy support is needed to give africa that chance. that goes for our research policy as well. i ask you to support the demand that the seventh framework programme for research be used to increase research on neglected tropical diseases. they kill millions of people every year.
the ppe group is in favour of the report. mr president, many of us thought that a democratic breakthrough had taken place in ethiopia in the context of the elections in may. oh, how wrong we were! in the days following the election, the meles government showed that it was having difficulty accepting the opposition's huge gains. laws were passed that limited the opposition's ability to act. the media is again tightly controlled by the regime, opposition politicians have been repeatedly arrested, peaceful demonstrations have been brutally suppressed by the security police, leading to over 40 deaths in june. the events of the past few days unfortunately confirm this picture. the negotiations between the opposition and the ruling party broke down a few days ago after the government refused to discuss what i believe are the obvious demands for democratic reforms. as a consequence, a number of opposition members chose not to attend the next sitting of parliament. the result was a law that stripped these members of their immunity. the opposition members who attended the meeting were denied the right to participate in the negotiations. we in this house know that such methods have nothing to do with democracy. the resolution we are discussing now looks at the events after the elections in may. our intention is not primarily to criticize the government and the ruling party. we intend to show that we are deeply concerned about developments in ethiopia, as commissioner michel has said, and to call on both sides to open negotiations and to seize the opportunity for peaceful and democratic development in the country which was strongly supported by the elections in may. the union is willing to contribute to positive developments in ethiopia, including support for poverty reduction, but this can only be achieved in conditions of respect for democratic principles and human rights. the meles government must address this, otherwise it risks losing its friends, which would ultimately be the poor people.
for the alde group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. i negotiated in the lisbon process the resolution adopted in the european parliament on behalf of the alde group. on that basis, we can be honest and say that the midterm results for the lisbon strategy are disastrous. very, very few of the key figures that have been put forward have actually been achieved. to complete the mission, we have only half the time left, but actually double the task. while we're sitting here wondering why we're lagging behind the united states in economic growth, you're sitting in the united states wondering why china's growth is twice that of the united states. i think the rapporteur has drawn up a very good report, but i am sorry to say that it is far too kind. we have agreed with the commission's proposal and supported the idea that this whole process should be based on europe having a knowledge-driven economy, but that would require both rather tough decisions and also living up to the ambitions of the budgetary discussions. what we can see is that the proposals coming from the commission, which have been supported by all the major groups in the european parliament, are not supported at all by the council of ministers. the budgetary discussions now being held on the budget plans are frankly embarrassing, because the proposals in question would, instead of increasing the research budget from the current level, in fact reduce it by more than 40 per cent. the same applies to the proposals on the eu level in the field of education. the debate has raised the issue of lifelong learning. this is of course a very important basis for all citizens to be able to benefit from this knowledge-based economy. i therefore ask the commission to explain why there are budget lines with enormous sums of money for lifelong learning that are not being used, that are locked up and impossible to search and that no one will have access to until this budget period has expired. i also wonder whether a very important issue in this context is the question of improving administration. i think that is very much a matter for the member states. as regards the report itself, i have a question on language training. language education is very important, but why focus on the languages of neighbouring countries? it is the freedom of students to choose which languages they wish to study, and it is not something that the commission and brussels should control. finally, our group will support this report. we think the rapporteur has done a very good job, but we are actually calling for a much tougher approach. we are not only asking parliament to meet its objectives, but also the commission to meet its objectives and to release the money for lifelong learning for application, and we are asking the council to answer the question of why in one breath it talks about a knowledge-driven economy and in the next does not allocate the necessary funds.
for the gue/ngl group. mr president, we in the left group believe that women should have equal and fair access to all health care. we cannot accept that women should have access to only certain health care. equal access to all health care, we believe. poverty affects all aspects of life, education, health, housing, social exclusion, but i would like to highlight the importance of women's ability to provide for themselves. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the social partners, the social partners and the social partners. women in the eu still earn on average only 85% of men's wages, and in some countries the gap is even wider. in addition to these unfair wage differences, women are referred to low-wage jobs, part-time work, poor employment conditions, etc. and this discrimination follows women throughout their lives, right up to retirement age, with pension systems that also discriminate against women. mr. speaker, women are not weak, but they are made weak.
for the verts/ale group. thank you, commissioner, for putting this proposal forward. i have long been waiting for driftnets to be banned in the baltic sea, the last sea in the eu where they are still allowed. i thank you for holding to that view even though there is criticism of this ban here in the european parliament at times. i should like to thank our rapporteur for his willingness to compromise on this issue as well. it was also wrong of the council not to involve the new member states in this process from the outset. it would have made the process easier if they had been included in the first stage. i don't think we can wait until the last of the tumbleweeds is dead before we can finally establish the scientific evidence, because then it's too late. we have seen studies showing the risks of drift nets and i think those studies well justify the precautionary principle here. the biodiversity of the baltic sea is very fragile. it is a very delicate sea and we must not risk upsetting this balance. i'm now awaiting your next initiative. a year ago we discussed the possibilities for countries to voluntarily introduce fishing stops and compensation to fishermen to improve the catching possibilities for future generations. i am waiting for rules from the commission to facilitate the possibility of voluntary cessation of fishing by countries wishing to increase the amount of fish in their waters for future profits. i hope we can discuss it in more detail.
mr president, the background for this request is as follows: the debate on the serious situation in ethiopia and the motion for a resolution have been in the making for quite some time. political groups agreed on a text last friday, but since then a lot of things have happened in ethiopia that are of great importance and ought to be referred to in the text. however, since the deadline had expired, there was no way we could really do that. the most serious thing that has happened is that the negotiations between the ruling party and the opposition parties broke down about a week ago, primarily because of the refusal of the ruling party and the prime minister to even discuss a number of issues related to the need for democratic reform. furthermore, the parliament in addis abbaba convened, and some of the opposition members were not taking part because of the breakdown in those negotiations. i believe i should explain the background to this, otherwise it does not make sense for members.
in writing. china is one of the fastest growing economies in the world. it is also the eu's largest trading partner, after the united states. it is therefore important for the eu to develop both trade and cooperation with this part of the world. our position on principle is that europe does not benefit from distancing itself from this growing economy of tariffs and quotas. china's growing economic power is affecting most industries. today's "textile wars" will therefore spread to other areas gradually. therefore, trying to protect unprofitable industries from international competition is not a sustainable solution. sweden has experienced this for 30 years, both in the coal and shipbuilding industries. the eu faces the choice between implementing centrally regulated protectionism or stimulating growth from below to meet growing international competition. unfortunately, several of the party groups are choosing the first route. we cannot therefore support the report. i also support the proposal to introduce a system of labelling the country of origin of products.
in writing. we believe that the eu has an important role to play in strengthening democracy and human rights in its neighbourhood. in the global work on peace, disarmament and human rights, we want to give priority to the un, which is better suited than the eu to solve these tasks. that is why we are voting against the resolutions on iran and ethiopia, even though we can support in principle several of their demands.
in writing. we are voting against this resolution by the european parliament. we fully agree with the swedish parliament's eu committee's decision of 7 november 2000 when it expressed its opinion on the question of increased eu coordination in the field of education. the eu parliamentary committee adopted a letter stating that the exclusive responsibility of the member states for the organisation of education and the content of education systems must be fully respected. the next day, eu education ministers met and adopted the amendment from the parliament's eu committee, presented by sweden's then education minister. we see no reason to reconsider this position.
in writing. we have abstained in the final vote on this report. this is a matter of the utmost importance - so important that it must be maintained as a political responsibility of the member states or their subordinate levels. the member states have exclusive responsibility for the organisation of teaching and the content of education systems. this applies in particular to the integration of immigrants and the organisation of education in both the immigrants' mother tongue and the language of the new country.
in writing. this report contains several proposals which we cannot support in any way. the question is why should the european parliament call on the member states and the commission to recognise the circus as part of european culture? the report also proposes the setting up of service units and information campaigns to facilitate the activities of circus companies. the report raises the question of the education of children in circus companies. free movement and access to education are important issues. however, this has to be dealt with in a larger and more cross-sectoral context than in a report like this.
in writing. the junilist group votes against this report because it is yet another example of how the european parliament, through its own-initiative report, wants to give the eu new areas of competence. the report also calls for the eu to be more involved in the fight against terrorism and to be more involved in the fight against organised crime. the report is a typical example of how the eu, through new 'models', 'tools', 'dialogue', 'working groups', various support programmes and eventually new institutions, is building bureaucracy and supranationality.
in writing. we voted for amendments nos 1, 3 and 5. we are in favour of a guaranteed decent standard of living. we are opposed to the introduction of state-relegated minimum wages. we interpret the term 'guaranteed income' in the above amendments not as meaning a certain guaranteed minimum wage but rather as a guaranteed reasonable standard of living.
in writing. the resolution on women and poverty in the eu contains several demands that are important to pay attention to, including equal access to health care for women and men and the fight against violence against women. it should be emphasised that this is the responsibility of the member states. we do not support the proposal for a "guaranteed minimum income". under the swedish labour market model, wages are determined through negotiations between trade unions and employers, not through legislation. in addition, the introduction of a minimum wage may lead to wage cuts in many low-wage groups, which are particularly likely to affect women. in view of the above, we choose to abstain from voting on the final vote on the resolution.
mr president, i would like to thank you. every year, thousands of students from all over europe have the opportunity to study abroad thanks to the programmes we are discussing. in fact, for many, this student exchange is the most tangible benefit of the whole eu project. studying abroad is not just about acquiring new knowledge and new experiences of a different culture. the dissemination of knowledge also contributes to strengthening the quality of european education, which is one of the cornerstones of the lisbon process. the vote tomorrow will simplify administrative procedures, which could lead to more schools and universities participating more actively in the programmes and encourage more students to study abroad. i am also glad that we social democrats have protested during this process against the proposals for cuts that the epp wanted to make in vocational training and that we have stood up for the continuation of good opportunities for vocational trainers to study abroad. indeed, vocational training makes a very important contribution to the eu economy and the functioning of the labour market. the committee on the environment, public health and consumer protection has also called for the introduction of a european standard for the quality of water. as regards funding, i would also very much like to see money for these programmes, but at the same time i think that all the budgetary issues should be dealt with at the same time so that we really have to prioritise what is important. i will therefore support the amendments to parliament's report on the whole budget, the böge report.
for the ind/dem group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. this report notes that cultural diversity is limited in several of the eu countries' respective film industries. it is clear that european films are having a hard time competing with their american counterparts. whether we view this fact as a problem or as something we have to accept, the question we should ask ourselves is this: is the eu supposed to deal with this type of policy issue or is this the responsibility of the member states? the junilists' answer is given, this is a question for the member states. if the member states wish to devote special resources to promoting the national film industry, they are of course fully entitled to do so. the eu, however, will not pursue specific programmes to promote the european film industry. if several eu countries consider it necessary to cooperate in this policy area, they can do so without the eu's involvement. intergovernmental agreements or cooperation between film companies may take place outside the eu. amendment no 15 refers to the eu constitution and stresses that the eu constitution emphasises the value of respecting cultural and linguistic diversity. however, this is not a legitimate reason to support eu programmes like this. the members of this house must accept that the people of france and the netherlands voted against the eu constitution, which has therefore fallen and is no longer relevant to refer to. the culture committee wants to invest over one billion euros in this programme. the eu does not need any more ambitious and costly projects. the eu should instead seek limited cooperation on important and genuinely crossborder issues.
for the gue/ngl group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. i would like to thank the rapporteur, mr hans blokland, for the very extensive and good work he has done on this report. of course, we would also have liked to have had more to gain from the compromise with the council. we have chosen not to sign it because we would have liked it to be even stronger, but at the same time it is highly doubtful whether a compromise would have been sufficient to achieve a better result than that achieved in these negotiations. as regards the legal basis, we would like to make it absolutely clear that this is an environmental issue. the aim must be to address the serious environmental problems associated with the trade in waste. we think it is completely unreasonable to treat hazardous waste as a commodity like others and we therefore believe that article 175 should be the sole legal basis. the rapporteur raised in his opening remarks the issue of non-compliance with the existing rules. we can probably expect some breaches of these rules as well. therefore, every effort should be made to ensure that they are actually observed. in this respect, the regulatory framework we have before us could have been stronger, but the greater the responsibility of the commission to ensure that these rules are actually implemented in practice. we hope you will devote your attention to this rather than questioning the legal basis. i also agree that there is a need for a much stronger international regulatory framework on ship scrapping and we need to return to this issue as soon as possible.
for the gue/ngl group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. when we discuss climate issues in this house, we tend to agree quite a bit. we tend to agree that we are the biggest environmental problem of our time, we tend to agree that we have to do more. now that we're here, now that we can do more, now that we can make sure that some of the most harmful greenhouse gases are eliminated or phased out, or at least limited. we know there are alternatives, and that's when we have to confess color. i fully support the rapporteur's claim that article 175 should be the legal basis. this is an environmental issue. it would be absurd to want to lower the environmental standards already in place in some of the eu member states when they should actually be tightened up. it's the hindrance to invention and innovation that you don't let countries get ahead. the objection is, but can it not prevent the single market? yes, it is possible, but my group believes that the right of democratic countries to tighten environmental standards if it is good for the environment - and it is - takes precedence over the right of companies to sell the exact same product across a certain market. we have seen unprecedented and brazen lobbying on this issue by a range of companies. several of these companies are attacking the democratic right of member states to tighten their legislation. these same companies often want to call themselves environmentally friendly or claim to have a modern environmental policy. it can only be summed up in one word, hypocrisy. mr president, i would like to thank you. i would like to thank mr doyle, who has done a very meritorious job with this report. i see it as our duty as legislators to stimulate and facilitate the environmentally destructive technologies. in the legislative field, we should also, if possible, avoid adopting a particular technical solution simply because it is the best for the environment at the for the verts/ale group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. commissioner mccreevy, you have launched a frontal attack on the swedish constitutional right to strike. you claim to accept the nordic model of collective agreements, but at the same time you want to undermine it with your legislation. you must realise that our legislative aim is to protect every worker against unfair working conditions, not swedish workers against latvian ones. you are making the most common mistake of all politicians at eu level, you believe that what worked in your country for a certain period of time will work for the eu in the future. your unilateral single market and growth policy as finance minister in ireland succeeded for a time in reducing unemployment and raising incomes. but it was due to certain temporary national contingencies. the success was achieved despite the lack of social protection, not because of it. in sweden, we have made tough transitions because of the security that social protection has given us. but the nordic model also has its own unique conditions which cannot be exported to the eu level by means of legislation. the right thing to do is to learn from each other but to allow legislation to be adapted to the conditions of each region. otherwise, we're going to have a centrally controlled "eurosclerosis". the eu is like a football team, what you do as a coach is you see that you have a good player in zlatan ibrahimovic. you conclude that all players should use his shoe size and that you should have 11 forwards on your team. with that tactic, our football team would lose to both india and china. instead, you have to see the potential for development of each player and what each player can bring to the team, without stifling the individual creativity, as long as they follow the basic rules of the game, only then there is a chance for the world cup title. mr president, i would like to thank you. the work to improve and clarify the all-important services directive is now at its best in parliament and the council of ministers. it is therefore strange and rather tragic that the directive is now being used as a baton in a fundamentally protectionist and nationalist debate in country after country. instead of standing up for the proposal and constructively trying to improve it, ministers in sweden and elsewhere have shamelessly exploited people's concerns about globalisation and jobs by painting the services directive as a major threat. social dumping is being presented as a vicious charm and an example of new vicious inventions from brussels. no one wants social dumping, but the services directive does not lead to social dumping, it does not threaten the swedish model or swedish collective agreements. the nearest hysterical tone of the social democratic trade unions and politicians here is irresponsible. the minister for economic affairs, mr östros, has lost his senses when he accuses the commission of intervening against a member state for a 'foreign company'. it cannot have escaped the attention of minister östros that the foreign company is latvian, that latvia is a member of the european union and that free movement is one of the cornerstones of eu cooperation. how is it that there are no problems when swedish construction workers go to germany and work for higher wages than in sweden but lower wages than the german construction workers? but it's a campaign in sweden, and then it's like this. free trade, competition, mobility of services is good for europe, it is good for sweden. sweden is one of the countries that stands to gain most from the removal of barriers to knowledge-intensive services. mr president, i would like to thank you. my colleague jan andersson of the socialist group pointed out just now that sweden did not introduce any transitional rules when the new members joined the european union. it was good that he said that, because he was boasting about his own loss, because he and his party were in the forefront of fighting free movement and wanted to introduce transitional rules, but they lost. (applause from the group of the european people's party) as a result, sweden is the country with free movement today. they were running a scare campaign about social tourism. we know the result: last year, the social tourism that jan andersson and other social democrats were threatening cost 18 000 euros in sweden. it's close to zero, but we've had a lot of people who have been able to contribute tax revenue and jobs to our country. we'll be glad you lost, because europe won. (applause from the group of the european people's party) this is not about the services directive. we are now witnessing a new propaganda of terror of the same kind as we heard a year ago. it is not about the services directive or chinese wages - it is a pure disrespect to our new members from central and eastern europe to threaten that freedom and the opportunity to work in europe will lead to chinese wages. nor is it a question of calling into question collective agreements in sweden, because the principle of the country of origin is not at issue at all. it is about allowing people from europe with collective agreements to work in a country where they also comply with the laws and regulations of that country. this is the kind of scare propaganda you are making, pure scare propaganda, directed against the services directive but ultimately directed against our new members from central and eastern europe who have made the european union richer. i can state one thing, namely that we swedish moderates will protect and guard the fact that we in sweden decide about our labour market and our rules, but we will also protect openness. i would just like to point out to the socialist group here that they are using the european union to legislate on the swedish labour market or the working time directive and the sunshine directive: we are to safeguard the swedish labour market and swedish freedom but we are also to safeguard the freedom of the european union. (applause from the group of the european people's party)
in writing. we have voted in favour of the ec-azerbaijan air service agreement, but this does not mean that we approve of the opening by azerbaijan of air services between the turkish-occupied north of cyprus and baku. we do not think that the opening of this air link is a reason to vote against this air service agreement. these are two different issues and negotiations with azerbaijan on the above mentioned air link must be taken in a different context.
in writing. this report strikes a balance between the internal market and the environment. in principle, we believe that the eu member states should decide for themselves whether or not to allow domestic companies to import waste from other countries. however, compliance with international agreements and the absence of environmental risks for other member states are necessary. of course, environmentally hazardous chemicals should not be transported via international waterways if alternative options are available. we have chosen to support the line of the committee on the environment, which represents a reasonable compromise between environmental considerations and compliance with the rules of the internal market. we therefore believe that international shipments of waste should be allowed, provided that the waste as such does not pose a significant environmental risk. in line with the above reasoning, we have opposed the rapporteur's position on some key points, such as that waste shipments should not be allowed if the waste can be handled in the country which produced it.
in writing. since the financial perspective for the period 2007-2013 was rejected by the european council, we cannot now deal with the action programme and its six sub-programmes for that period. we are in favour of the erasmus programme, for example, but there are other parts of the subprogramme proposals which need to be examined carefully and whether they are justified on the basis that each member state is responsible under the treaty for the organisation and content of education and training systems. we have therefore voted against the report today, as we believe that the financial perspective for the period 2007 to 2013 must first be clear.
in writing. since the financial perspective for 2007-2013 has been rejected by the european council, we consider it premature to discuss the establishment of the 'youth in action' programme at this stage. at the present time, it is unreasonable to support a total budget of eur 915 million (or eur 1 128 million depending on which proposal wins the vote) for this programme. the programme's content may also be called into question. the rapporteur states in his explanatory statement that it 'contributes significantly to an active citizenship of young people in society and deepens their sense of belonging to the eu, which can provide a significant european added value'. we question whether the programme really makes any substantial contribution to this objective. international youth exchanges are a positive thing, but they must be solved financially through the efforts of civil society or through the provision of funds by the member states. we do not think that the eu should contribute money from its budget in this area. we have therefore voted against the report today, as we believe that the financial perspective for the period 2007 to 2013 must first be clear.
in writing. i support voluntary service and youth programmes in/for the world, especially cooperation with non-eu countries. this creates a real sense of connection and understanding of the world around us. however, i cannot vote for the proposed increase of €200 million, particularly because the youth for europe contribution is being increased. youth for europe aims to create a new form of euro-nationalism, something we should have learned from history to avoid. i therefore abstain, as the proposal contains many positive as well as many negative elements.
in writing. of course, cultural matters are very important. junilista believes that cultural policy should, in principle, be managed by the member states. however, in the case of european cultural heritage, it may be justified to deal with certain issues at community level. however, individual expenditure items should not be discussed before the adoption of the eu's long-term budget. that is why the junilista has voted against this report.
, in writing. - i have decided to abstain in the final vote on the report on the 2007-2013 cultural programme. it is certainly important to promote intercultural dialogue, crossborder cultural projects and cultural exchanges which increase understanding of the different cultures in europe and which help to give concrete content to european citizenship. but the most concrete element in the european parliament's report is a very large increase in budget appropriations compared with what the commission is proposing. since i cannot support this budget increase, i have decided to abstain from voting on the report. i think that the commission's proposal on the amount of money to be budgeted for the cultural programme is quite sufficient.
in writing. i've always been a cultural advocate, but this program takes resources from national cultural budgets. the commission's proposal for a budget of €408 million is already an increase of 72%, but the ep's proposal for a budget of €600 million is too much. amendment no 29 on strengthening the sense of european citizenship is an example of the negative euronationalism that is constantly flourishing in the committee on culture. in addition, the programme has an excessive administrative burden, something that cultural workers in all countries agree is an obstacle to creative development. i therefore vote against this proposal.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the junilists welcome romania and bulgaria as members of the european union in the same way as we welcome turkey, the countries of the western balkans and subsequently ukraine and hopefully belarus. the question is the timetable, because it is important. i would like to warn of the problems facing the eu if we take in a very short time a large number of poor and economically underdeveloped countries with little experience of democracy and the rule of law and with major problems of corruption. the enlargement of the union to 10 countries, which junilistan has been a strong supporter of, is already causing problems. now, bringing poor countries like romania and bulgaria into an eu that continues to be dragged down by an unreasonable agricultural policy, a poorly managed structural policy and that is struggling to prevent waste and corruption in its own system is dangerous for the future of the european project. we must ask ourselves at what stage a country should become a full member. is progress most rapid when a candidate country is working towards approval or after it has entered? i believe that the pressure to improve democracy, human rights, the judiciary and public administration and to establish a functioning market economy is strongest before the accession negotiations have begun and secondly before the country has been accepted as a member. the commission's report on the situation in romania and bulgaria is not convincing on these various points. we find a key point, but progress has been insufficient in a number of areas, and the commission gives long lists of what has not been implemented, such as infrastructure, organised crime, tax systems, the treatment of minorities. in this context, the membership of both countries should be suspended for the time being. this is in the long-term interest of both the european union and the two candidate countries.
the commission is not prepared to accept this. the enlargement and unification of europe will of course not be complete without our friends romania and bulgaria. they have a natural place in the european community and we look forward to their accession shortly. in the liberal group we are delighted to have our colleagues, our observers here, to get to know them and to prepare each other for the coming membership. we are impressed by the progress made in both countries, but at the same time we must of course examine this progress, the implementation and the remaining problems. the commissioner pointed out some of these problems, areas requiring further action with which there is a certain urgency. we must be vigilant in the field of human rights, which is an area to which parliament in general and the liberal group in particular always give special attention. we owe it to the people of the candidate countries, and we owe it to ourselves, to be able to defend european values. corruption is a major concern, and the roma, europe's largest minority, live marginalized lives with high unemployment, high rates of ill health and serious social problems, in romania and bulgaria as in many other european countries. discrimination and racial violence unfortunately still exist and it is all too common that it is on the part of the authorities. this is of course not acceptable. another concern mentioned by some colleagues is that of children, especially those in institutions, including those with intellectual disabilities. there are alarming signs of open neglect of these children and this is of course unworthy of a member state. i hope that the commission will step up its efforts to help romania and bulgaria to overcome these and other problems so that it is possible - as i still believe and hope - to do so in the context of membership in 2007.
madam president, i would like to thank you. i would like to thank commissioner rehn for a well-balanced report. unfortunately, i do not think that the debate on enlargement is always balanced. those who want to slow down enlargement talk about the danger of taking in poor countries, that the eu treaty referendums changed the eu climate and that europe has become colder. they say that the referendums were not really about the treaty itself but about other things. and that is true, it has been about other things because we political leaders in europe did not show enough political leadership, because we were not able to explain that the referendums on the treaty were really about the treaty and not about anything else. let us now make sure that we do not make the same mistake, that the debate today on the accession of romania and bulgaria to the european union is really about that and nothing else. we must show that we have visions and plans for enlargement. enlargement is not about taking in new members haphazardly when we please and when public opinion in our countries allows it. enlargement is about democracy and freedom in europe, but above all about european security - i am surprised that nobody mentioned it today. it would be wrong to see the accession of bulgaria and romania to the eu as an isolated event, because many challenges await us in the coming years, such as the debate on the status of kosovo, the referendum, the independence of montenegro, the continuing discussions with bosnia on serbia, macedonia's application for membership. in this very uncertain security situation, it would be wrong to postpone the accession of romania and bulgaria without being absolutely necessary. no one here, nor in romania and bulgaria, is suggesting that we should include a country that does not meet the criteria, but we should not disqualify anyone in advance either. if europe has become colder since the referendums, then it is our task to warm it up. if europe has become more selfish and closed, then it is our task to make it more generous and open, that is what makes the difference between those who form opinion and those who create opinion, that is what makes us the political leaders of europe who take responsibility.
many eu citizens who enter israel are subjected to very brutal and degrading treatment, particularly if they are of arab origin or if they are active in solidarity with the democratic development of palestine. recently, a young swedish woman who had travelled to israel was subjected to very violent treatment. she was threatened with death by the israeli border guards and she was rejected in the most brutal ways because she has worked for a democratic and free palestine. in your relations with the israelis, do you raise the question of how they treat eu citizens when they enter israel?
for the pse group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. i thank rapporteur mojca drčar murko and commissioner markos kyprianou for the willingness to reach agreement that you have shown in the course of this work. the socialist group naturally welcomes the commission's proposal to restrict the use of substances in jelly jelly in mini-cups, which have led to tragic choking accidents. we also welcome the commission's proposal to regulate the amount of nitrates and nitrites on the basis of additives in meat, for example, instead of residues as is the case now. we believe that this will lead to a safer control for consumers. we want these restrictions to come into force as soon as possible and that is why we have been keen to find a quick settlement with the council. we are also pleased that the agreement with the council means that the use of soybean hemicellulose is restricted in order not to make things more difficult for allergy sufferers. on the other hand, we would have liked to see lower maximum levels for nitrates and nitrites. we would also have liked to see a ban on the use of antibiotics in foodstuffs because of the risk of increasing resistance and we would have liked to see a more thorough examination of the risks of the sweetener aspartame, but on these points we were opposed by a majority in the committee. now that the commission has expressed in writing and in this house that it takes our concerns about the long-term use of aspartame and antibiotics in food seriously, we will support the compromise reached with the council and therefore vote against the other amendments. however, we would like to call on the commission to return to these issues as soon as possible, to revise these directives in depth, taking into account the need to limit the use of additives in food and to take greater account of the situation of vulnerable groups. more and more people are rightly expressing concern about the amount of additives in food and its long-term consequences. the eu must take these concerns very seriously.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the parliament's draft budget for 2006 provides for an increase to 1.04 per cent of the average gross national income of the member states. i regret that parliament is trying to increase expenditure on a large number of items without proposing corresponding and sufficient savings in other areas. the council, i.e. the governments of the member states, proposes that expenditure be limited to 1.01% of gross national income for 2006. i support that motion. the member states are responsible for setting the framework for spending. this is a level that the member states and the net payers have been willing to accept. my basic view is that parliament should have a great deal of influence over priorities within this framework, but the political responsibility in the various countries for the amount of taxes to be collected lies with the national governments. we should not try to stretch the budget in parliament all the time - that is going too far. parliament must confine itself to the main task of setting priorities within the framework set by the member states.
in writing. we share the view that a continuous review of the legislation in force is necessary. the question is, however, at what political level the legislation should be established. we are of the opinion that the food additives to be authorized should be decided on by the national food authorities of the member states. if the food authority of a country considers that certain specific additives should be allowed in the preparation and sale of a food, we see no reason for an eu directive to affect this assessment. food safety and public health are of paramount importance. they should therefore only be handled by eu authorities where there are tangible and cross-border benefits to be gained from doing so. there are good reasons to believe that this report, and similar legislative proposals, will further strengthen eu authorities such as the european food safety authority (efsa). we have voted in favour of the amendments which emphasize that this is a minimum directive, and which thus make it clear that national flexibility should be allowed. we have voted against the report as a whole. we are confident that the national food authorities of the member states are fully competent to handle this important matter satisfactorily. our position naturally presupposes that goods are labelled with the country of origin. this is to enable consumers to make an active and informed choice.
in writing. the junilist is in favour of increased cooperation and information exchange between member states to combat organised crossborder crime. however, decisions on the harmonisation of criminal and procedural law should be taken only by unanimity of the member states and subject to scrutiny by national parliaments. the determination of the nature of the offence, the mitigating circumstances and the criminal value of the offence shall not be a matter for the european parliament.
in writing. this resolution deals with a highly transnational issue. there is therefore good reason for the eu countries to coordinate the measures taken and the political decisions taken. we have therefore voted in favour of the resolution as a whole. however, we would like to stress that eu action in isolation is insufficient to address the challenges posed by the global influenza pandemic. it is therefore of the utmost importance that the eu acts in cooperation with the world health organisation (who). we question certain points in the report, such as the need for the commission to develop a comprehensive communication strategy together with the european centre for disease prevention and control (ecdc). we assume that the eu countries are fully capable of communicating with their citizens on their own, without the assistance of eu bodies. we believe that internationally recognised organisations such as the who should be working on global health issues. several amendments ignore this, and instead emphasise the eu's international role. we criticise the fact that the european parliament is using an important political issue in this way to strengthen the eu and its international political role.
in writing. the own-initiative report is part of the process of establishing a european immigration policy, with a single procedure for all member states. the junilista group, however, believes that member states should be free to determine the form of their economic migration policy and that it should not be for the european parliament to lay down the rules applicable in this area. we believe that economic migration should not be seen as a problem requiring harmonisation at european level - on the contrary, member states should be allowed to deal with these issues independently. for example, a member state wishing to allow generous rules on economic migration should be able to do so in accordance with the principle of institutional competition.
for the alde group. president. - the debate is closed. as we approach the 10th anniversary, there is every reason to evaluate the barcelona process. the world today looks very different from what it did 10 years ago. we have had enlargement with, for example, cyprus and have started negotiations with turkey and croatia, the neighbourhood policy has developed and we have seen how democratization in many of our partner countries has progressed and in some cases unfortunately also backslidden. the fight against terrorism has become increasingly important. the war in iraq and the difficult process that the country is now going through on its way to democracy also affect relations with the region. we have iran and the problems of nuclear fuel policy there, the gaza strip and the palestinian elections, but at the same time a stagnation in the peace process. the exciting events in lebanon and syria and the continuing tragic situation in africa's last colony, western sahara, show that there is still a great deal to be done in the region. in this context, it is natural to reconsider and discuss the barcelona process. it has created structures, very important ones, and a form of closer cooperation between us. the association agreements with all the countries have led to closer exchanges, trade, projects, many meeting places and it has created a long-term commitment for the whole union. the proof is that here are members from all over europe, not just the south, debating tonight. we have a common history and culture and also common problems and solutions: terrorism, migration, growth, development, trade, environment, energy, yes, there are many. but above all, the future is about human rights and democracy. this is the most important point and here the barcelona process has failed in many ways. e association agreements contain human rights clauses but they are not used because we do not have good mechanisms to deal with serious violations in the countries with which we cooperate. interest is also quite limited at times. this is the great challenge for the barcelona process in the future. we must restructure the whole cooperation so that it becomes a framework for a gigantic democratization effort throughout the region. nothing contributes more to stability than the transition to democracy. the middle east is the most unforgiving region in the world, and this is creating a breeding ground for fanaticism, frustration and perhaps even terrorism. that is why we must, within the barcelona process, devise strategies for comprehensive democratization efforts, support for dissidents, civil society, the legal community, freedom of expression, etc. the means of this are, of course, trade, exchanges, educational programmes and cultural cooperation. the barcelona process must thus become a kind of alternative to countries which can never become members of the eu. it must provide tangible benefits in return for our demands for reform. if we succeed in this, we will make a historic contribution. in conclusion, i would like to commend my colleague, mr jäätteenmäki, on a very good report.
in writing. we want the budget to contribute to the sustainable social, economic and environmental development of the eu in line with the lisbon strategy. we have therefore voted in favour of the amendments which give priority to research, development and education. however, we do not want to support the amendments which provide more money for direct agricultural aid, since this aid is already over-extended. this support results in inefficient agriculture that harms the environment and, by dumping surpluses, contributes to widening the gap between the world's poor and rich. we also do not want to give money to tobacco farming, which directly contributes to public health problems. we believe that this aid should be reduced rapidly and that it should be abolished in the near future. the money thus saved can be used, among other things, for measures to reduce tobacco use and dependence. we want to see a reform of direct aid to sugar growers, since we cannot support a production which is largely superfluous in the long term. we do not want to leave farmers in developing countries without a livelihood, and that is why we voted for transitional support to compensate them until they have changed their production.
in writing. the junilist believes that the eu budget should be limited to 1.00% of the average gnp of the member states and therefore supports the council's position in principle. the 2006 budget is the last budget in the current financial perspective, and it is particularly unfortunate that the european parliament is trying to move its positions forward in the forthcoming negotiations by proposing substantial increases. we have therefore voted against all the increases proposed by the european parliament, while welcoming the few savings proposed by the committee on budgets or by individual members in the form of amendments. there are other unfortunate budget lines, but junilista particularly regrets the high level of support for the eu's agricultural policy, the cohesion fund, the fisheries sector and the budget lines which support various forms of information campaigns.
in writing. we believe that the eu has an important role to play in its own area in promoting democracy and human rights. however, both resolutions concern countries and territories which cannot be considered as being adjacent to the eu. it is clear that the aim is to highlight the role of the eu in foreign and security policy. other organisations are suitable for this purpose, such as the un, the osce and the council of europe. the latter two organisations should be given priority in the monitoring of elections and the protection of human rights in europe. in view of the above, we shall abstain in the final vote on the two resolutions. however, we have chosen to support some proposals which, in our opinion, are outside the eu's foreign and security policy. this includes proposals to establish a free trade area between the eu and the mediterranean by 2010, and to develop cooperation on civil and environmental protection.
writer. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. the council made a good decision on the arms embargo on 3 october. uzbekistan does not need weapons, they can be used to oppress its own people. it is good that we are today calling for an independent inquiry into what happened in abyssinia. the media is tightly controlled and opposition and grassroots movements are prevented from meeting. the demands we place on president karimov shouldn't be so difficult to meet. he only needs to comply with his own constitution, which states that there should be freedom of movement, freedom of association and assembly and that the courts should be independent. it is not an unreasonable demand that a president follow the constitution of his own country, and that is what we are demanding here today.
